## Status and ownership

- Progress is in `build/GHYE6S/report.json`; `python3 tools/rea/finalize.py` prints the totals. The latest numbers are in `git log`. At handoff it was about 6,950 / 10,159 functions and about 27% of code.
- **You own the game code**: every `auto/text_*` unit EXCEPT the library units below.
- **Library units are owned by another agent. Do not edit them:**
  - `auto/text_800B2E70` (Dolphin SDK, `GC/1.2.5n`)
  - `auto/text_800E40A0` (MSL / Metrowerks runtime, `GC/2.0`)
  - `src/sdk/*` (SDK EXI/SI/DB units split out of text_800E40A0)
  - `auto/text_80273E10` (asm-heavy low-level SDK)
  - `auto/text_800DAA80` (MetroTRK)
  - `bink/*` (RAD Bink, `ProDG/3.5`; was `auto/text_802752DC`, split into 16 TUs)

  If you need a symbol or declaration from one of them, declare it locally in your own unit.
- Game code compiles with **GC/1.3.2**, flags `-O4,p -inline auto` (see `configure.py`). Some units are C++ (`-Cpp_exceptions on`) and some also need `-use_lmw_stmw on`.

## Hard rules

1. `build/GHYE6S/main.dol` must stay **byte-identical or register-equivalent** to the original: `python3 tools/equivcheck.py` must say OK before every commit (see "Register-equivalent matches" below), and `config/GHYE6S/build.sha1` must hold the hash of the DOL you built, so plain `ninja` passes.
2. Commit in batches. End commit messages with the project's standard trailer (see existing commits).
3. On the machine set up by decomp.wtf (toolchains under `~/.local/share/decompnet`), never run bare `python3 configure.py`: it would switch `build.ninja` to downloaded tools under `build/`. ninja re-runs configure automatically when `configure.py` changes. If you must regenerate by hand:
   ```
   T=$HOME/.local/share/decompnet/toolchains
   ~/.local/share/uv/tools/decompnet/bin/python configure.py --wrapper $T/wibo/wibo --compilers $T/mwcc \
     --binutils $T/gc-wii-binutils --dtk $T/dtk/dtk --objdiff $T/objdiff-cli/objdiff-cli --sjiswrap $T/sjiswrap/sjiswrap.exe
   ```
   On any other machine, plain `python3 configure.py` (as in README.md) downloads the tools into `build/` and is correct; `ninja build/binutils` fetches objdump for the scripts.
4. Apply a candidate exactly once. Applying, then `git checkout`, then re-applying leaves untracked `.cpp` files and creates duplicate definitions. To roll back use `git checkout -- . && git clean -fdq -e 'zz_q*' src/`.
5. The judge writes temp files `src/auto/zz_*`. They are gitignored; never commit them.

## Register-equivalent matches (policy since 2026-11-11)

A function whose **only** difference from the original is which registers hold the values counts as matched: its
unit is set to `Matching` in `configure.py` and its C is linked. `main.dol` is then no longer byte-identical to the
retail DOL, so the sha1 check alone is not the safety net any more. Exact matches are still preferred; when a
register-equivalent function later becomes exact, remove it from the list.

- `config/GHYE6S/regequiv.txt` is the allowlist: one function name per line (`fn_80228D7C`), `#` comments.
- `python3 tools/equivcheck.py` compares `build/GHYE6S/main.dol` with `orig/GHYE6S/sys/main.dol` and exits nonzero
  unless: the size, the section table and every byte outside `.text` are identical (exception: extab, next bullet); every differing `.text` word is
  inside a listed function; in those functions every instruction has the same opcode and the same immediates,
  displacements, branch targets and condition bits; and the register fields are a renaming of **live ranges**
  (since 2026-11-11, second version): every operand that is read must be reached by the same definitions (same
  instruction positions) in both versions, so a value may be in r29 in one region and r30 in another, but two values
  that are live together can never share a register. The two sources of a commutative instruction (add, and, or, xor,
  mullw, fadd, fmul, the multiplicands of fmadd, ...) may be swapped. Entry values (parameters), call arguments, what
  a callee may read, call results, r3/r4/f1 and the callee-saved registers at a return, r1/r2/r13 and implicitly used
  registers must be in the same register number. Since 2026-11-11 (wUK) a plain copy (`mr`, `fmr`) hands on the
  definitions of its source instead of being one (`addi r3,..; mr r31,r3` equals `addi r31,..; mr r3,r31`), with a
  stale mark when the source's site runs again; entry values and call results are not forwarded. Reordered instructions are still a FAIL. The exact rules and
  assumptions are in the docstring. It prints one `PASS`/`FAIL` line per differing function.
- `--strict` applies the first version of the rule (one renaming for the whole function). `--selftest` re-checks the
  listed functions and makes sure corrupted copies (an operand reading another register, swapped operands of
  subf/fdiv/cmpw, a moved call argument) are rejected; run it after changing the script.
- `python3 tools/equivcheck.py --list auto/text_8019A7D0 [unit:fn | fn ...]` tells, without linking, whether the
  functions of a unit WOULD pass, by comparing `build/GHYE6S/src/<unit>.o` with `build/GHYE6S/obj/<unit>.o`. A unit
  reported `QUALIFIES` has only exact and register-equivalent functions. The link can still fail the real check for
  data or extab reasons.
- **extab register operands** (2026-11-11, wUM): C++ exception actions that destroy/delete an object held in a register name
  that register (DELETEPOINTER 0x0A / DELETEPOINTERCOND 0x0B: u16 operand, flag 0x80 first / 0x40 second operand), so a
  register rename shows up in `extab`. `equivcheck.py` accepts differing extab bytes only in that u16 of a DELETEPOINTER/
  DELETEPOINTERCOND action of a function listed in `regequiv.txt` (decoded via extabindex -> PC ranges -> action chain;
  entries it cannot decode must be identical) and only if, at every call inside the action's PC range, the register named
  in ours holds exactly the definitions the original register holds (the same rule d) test); the unchanged register
  operands of listed functions are checked the same way. Padding, flags, cond/stack operands, extabindex stay identical
  (use `extab_padding`). `--selftest` covers consistent / wrong-register / changed-byte cases. Linked this way:
  text_801E26B0, text_80212DA4.
- To link such a unit: set it `Matching`, add its register-equivalent functions to `regequiv.txt`, `ninja
  build/GHYE6S/main.dol`, `python3 tools/equivcheck.py`, then `sha1sum build/GHYE6S/main.dol >
  config/GHYE6S/build.sha1` and commit `build.sha1` in the same commit. Never update `build.sha1` without a passing
  equivcheck.
- Since the per-live-range version, `--list` accepts most pure register differences (54 of the 89 drafts whose
  disassembly differs in register numbers only, against 6 before). What still fails: two instructions in swapped
  order, a temporary left in r3/r4/f1 at a return or in an argument register at a call, and anything that is not a
  rename.
- `report.json` / objdiff still show a linked register-equivalent function below 100%.

### Schedule-equivalent matches (EXPERIMENT, branch `sched-equiv` only, wYA 2026-11-12)

On this local branch a function may additionally differ from the original in the **order of independent instructions
inside basic blocks**. Such functions are listed in `config/GHYE6S/schedequiv.txt` (separate from `regequiv.txt`) and
`tools/equivcheck.py` checks them with rule f) (docstring and the comment above `sched_info()`):

- same length, same basic blocks, every branch / call / return at the same position with the same target;
- our block is a permutation of the original block's instructions up to live-range renaming: the checker puts our
  instructions back into the original order and that function must pass the register rule d) unchanged (ABI rules at
  calls and returns, extab rule e);
- the permutation may only exchange instructions of ours that do not depend on each other: registers (GPR, FPR, CR
  fields, XER[CA], FPSCR class/condition bits; a read stays between the write it sees and the next write, a live write
  stays after all earlier writes), memory (a store stays ordered with every load/store unless both use the same base
  register with the same value and non-overlapping `[off, off+size)`, which includes two different r1 slots; indexed
  accesses are never disjoint; in a function that builds a 0xCC00/0xCD00/0xE000 address loads keep their order too),
  and barriers nothing may cross: branches, calls, mtspr/mfspr (mflr/mtlr/mtctr), mfmsr/mtmsr, mfcr/mtcrf, CR logic,
  sync/isync, cache instructions, lmw/stmw, OE forms, mffs/mtfsf/mtfsb, float record forms.
- A stack store against a load through another pointer was NOT accepted by the first version (see the two memory
  rules below).
- `--list` reports `schedequiv ...` / `UNIT ...: QUALIFIES-SCHED`; before linking, relocated displacements are unknown
  and local relocation targets are not compared by name, so the linked check is the authority. `--selftest` covers
  hand-made cases, real pairs and mutations (dependent pair swapped, load above an aliasing store, instruction across
  a block boundary or a call, changed operand/immediate must be rejected; independent swaps must be accepted).
- **Memory rule 'global' (wYA follow-up, 2026-11-12, `SchedCtx`)**: an access to this function's OWN FRAME is
  disjoint from an access to a GLOBAL. Own frame: r1-based, constant offset, completely inside `[0, N)`, at a point
  where r1 is the function's frame pointer (exactly one `stwu r1,-N(r1)`; r1 otherwise only written by
  `addi r1,r1,N` / `lwz r1,0(r1)`; forward dataflow over the CFG, anything else switches the rule off). Offsets at or
  above N (caller's frame, stack parameters, LR slot) and negative ones stay ordered. Global: base r13 / r2 (never
  written in the function; the bases are read from `__init_registers`, and base+offset must lie inside a DOL section
  or the DOL's bss range, where the stack is not), or a base register that holds, from a `lis` / `lis`+`addi` in the
  same block, a constant address inside those ranges. With `--list` (unlinked): an `@sda21` access, or `sym@l(rX)`
  with rX from a relocated `lis sym@ha`.
- **Memory rule 'pointer' (wYA follow-up, 2026-11-12)**: an own-frame access (as above) is disjoint from an access
  through ANY other base register (indexed forms included) only if no frame address can exist outside r1: in the
  original and in ours r1 appears nowhere except as the base of displacement loads/stores (lmw/stmw included), in the
  prologue `stwu r1,-N(r1)` and in `addi r1,r1,N` / `lwz r1,0(r1)`. One `addi rX,r1,..`, `mr rX,r1`, `add ..,r1,..`,
  `stw r1,..`, r1 as an index, anywhere in the function, and all pointer accesses stay ordered with the frame
  (`--selftest`: "frame address taken ..."). In practice this rule adds no candidate at the moment: the drafts that
  reorder a stack store against a pointer load all take the address of a local.
- **r4 / f1 at a return (wYA, 2026-11-12, `Program.retuse`)**: rule d) used to require r3, r4 and f1 to hold the same
  definitions at every return. r3 still does. r4 and f1 are now required only when something may read them after the
  return: a caller's own instruction, a later callee that has them among its inputs (every indirect callee counts),
  a caller that returns them on to such a reader, a caller in code the checker cannot analyse, or - when the
  function's address is taken (an aligned word with its address anywhere in the DOL outside extabindex, or a
  `lis` + `addi`/`ori` of one function that builds it) - any indirect call site that reads them. In practice this is
  conservative: a function whose callers go on to a virtual call without setting r4 still needs r4 (flips
  fn_801F4C84; fn_801A8C24 is address-taken and fn_801B1D54's callers are virtual methods that return right after it).
- To link: unit `Matching`, function name into `schedequiv.txt`, then as for register-equivalent units.
  Candidates: `/home/mi/Decomp/hm-work/wYA/candidates.txt`. Do not merge into main before the user decides.

## Tools (tools/decomp_agent/)

Copy them into a scratch dir and run from there. Scripts locate each other through `$DECOMP_SCRATCH`:
```
export DECOMP_SCRATCH=/tmp/decomp-scratch; mkdir -p $DECOMP_SCRATCH
cp tools/decomp_agent/* $DECOMP_SCRATCH/; cp $DECOMP_SCRATCH/game_skip.txt $DECOMP_SCRATCH/skip.txt
```
These scripts and the `tools/rea/*.py` ones hardcode the original checkout (`/home/mi/Decomp/hauntedmansion-ghye6s`) and
toolchain (`~/.local/share/decompnet/toolchains/...`). On another machine, sed those paths in your scratch copies to your
checkout and to `build/tools/{wibo,objdiff-cli,dtk}`, `build/compilers/` and `build/binutils/` (copy `apply_q.py`,
`finalize.py`, `c2cpp.py`, `link.py` and `layout.py` too, keeping finalize's state files in `tools/rea`).
| tool | purpose |
|---|---|
| `q.py UNIT FN cand.c [cpp]` | Local judge: compiles the candidate standalone (game flags) and diffs FN against the built object. Prints `MATCH` or a diff. Env `QV=<ver>` (bare, e.g. `1.3.2`, `2.0`), `QX=<extra flags>`. Parallel-safe. It compares relocation types, not symbol names: a candidate that calls the wrong function or ctor still prints `MATCH` (the layout check catches it later), so check names, especially in sibling copies. |
| `qa3.sh DIR LIST [jobs]` | Parallel judge over `fn unit` lines. Picks `DIR/FN.*`; a `.lmw` suffix adds `-use_lmw_stmw on`. Prints `ok …` / `FAIL …` lines. |
| `unitcheck.py UNIT FN cand.c [QV]` | Merges the candidate into a temp copy of the unit, as apply does, and compiles the whole unit. Catches declaration conflicts the standalone judge misses. Run it on every C match (it compiles `src/UNIT.c` with `-lang=c`, so it reports ERROR for C++ candidates; build those instead). |
| `dumpasm2.py N skip.txt` | Compact asm of the N smallest unmatched functions (excludes library units). Writes to stdout. |
| `applyres.sh res…` | Applies `ok FN UNIT path` lines through `tools/rea/apply_q.py`. FAIL lines go to skip.txt. |
| `addctors.py res…` | Adds the `.ctors` entry for sinit functions whose unit has `.ctors` in splits.txt. Without it the layout check fails. |
| `families.py` / `sibling.py` / `sibimm.py` | Groups unmatched functions by normalized asm; copies a matched sibling's source with symbols and immediates remapped. Large free wins. |
| `relocs.py` | Per-instruction relocation helper used by the above. |
| `ps.py FN` | REA saved pseudocode lookup. It needs `pseudo.json`, an index built from `tools/rea/rea/*.json`. Rebuild it or use the REA MCP tools directly. |
| `permdecl.py`, `perm.py` | Brute-force declaration-order permutations for register-allocation misses. |
| `voltry.py` | Retries failures with float externs made `volatile`. |

After applying: `ninja`, then remove the touched units from `tools/rea/c2cpp_rejected.txt` (finalize skips units listed there), then `python3 tools/rea/finalize.py` (converts, re-verifies from a clean build, links), then the sha check, then commit.

## Workflow loop

1. `python3 -I $DECOMP_SCRATCH/dumpasm2.py 60 $DECOMP_SCRATCH/skip.txt > batch.txt`. Small functions (<150 B) are mostly done; most of what is left is 150 to 1500 bytes. Use REA pseudocode for those.
2. Write candidates `DIR/FN.c` or `FN.cpp` (add `.lmw` if the target uses stmw/lmw). Make them self-contained: declare all prototypes and externs locally.
3. Run `qa3.sh DIR list`. Fix the failures in a second directory (`q.py` shows the diff).
4. Apply, then build, finalize, check the sha and commit, as above. Add the batch to skip.txt.
5. Every few batches re-run `families.py` + `sibling.py`; newly matched functions unlock siblings.

## Matching cookbook (GC/1.3.2)

- **`li` vs `mr` for a zero/counter init (found 2026-11-11, wVA; scheduler notes in `/home/mi/Decomp/hm-work/SCHEDULER.md` "LI_VS_MR"):** this is not scheduling, it is MWCC backend CSE. It rewrites `li 0` into `mr` only for COMPILER-MADE values (the hidden `i*size` counter of an `a[i]` access, loop-hoisted constants); a user statement `off = 0;` keeps its `li`. Target has `li` where we emit `mr` -> write that offset/counter as an explicit variable initialised in source (`u32 off = 0; ... off += 12;`). Target has `mr` where we emit `li` -> index with `a[i]` and let the compiler derive the counter (or store a literal `0` inside the loop instead of keeping a `zero` variable).

- **Register allocation priority (found 2026-11-11 with mwcc-debugger, GC/2.6 as a stand-in for 1.3.2):** virtual registers are coloured in DESCENDING vreg number. Named locals and params get LOW numbers in declaration order; unnamed temporaries (sub-expressions, values assigned straight into struct fields) get higher numbers and are coloured FIRST. So to move a value to an earlier/lower callee-saved register, turn a named local into an unnamed temp (e.g. `b.y = tgt->y;` instead of `f32 by = tgt->y; b.y = by;`), or reorder declarations. Matched `fn_8023BE44` (a0f0a6c6). Tooling: `/home/mi/Decomp/hm-work/wVD/HOWTO.md` (mwcc-debugger dumps the priority list), details in `/home/mi/Decomp/hm-work/SCHEDULER.md` (Claude workers' dir; ask if you can't see it).

- **Allocate-then-construct without the extra `mr r0,r3`** (found 2026-11-11 on `fn_8003BC2C`, collaborator range): write it as `q = (C *)nw(n); if (q) ctor(q); p->f = q;` with a `void` ctor. That gives the target's `mr. r31,r3; beq; bl ctor; stw r31,...`. Minimal repro: `/home/mi/Decomp/hm-work/wVB/repro/EXTRA_MR_2*` (Claude workers' scratch dir; ask if you can't see it).

- **Virtual calls**: vtable slot = (disp − 8) / 4. If the vptr is at offset N, model the class as `struct V { u8 pad[N]; virtual …; }`.
- **Register order**: the first-declared local tends to get r31, and parameter registers follow first use. Try permutations (`perm.py`). Reusing a parameter (`p = p->next`) reproduces register reuse. Declare all locals at the top for sequential loops.
- **Branches**: `beq x; b y` comes from a ternary or a single-case `switch`. Two compares on the same cr come from an inline helper with its own check.
- **Constants**: repeated reloads of the same float global mean `extern volatile f32`. A float kept in a register means the source used a local copy.
- **Copies**: interleaved load/store mean `__restrict` pointers. A two-word `lwz/lwz/stw/stw/lwz/stw` is a struct with an array member assigned by value. Use 12-byte struct copies (`T12`) for vec3-style copies.
- **Indexed fields**: `mulli; addi off; add base` or `add; lwz off(r)` means member-array indexing through a struct type (`((S*)p)->arr[i]`, `&(*(T**)(p+X))[i].field`), not manual `p+i*K+off`. Plain `arr[i]` reproduces `li r5,0; li r6,0; mr r4,r5` loop setups.
- **Masks**: `& 0xFB` gives `andi.`; `&= ~4` gives `rlwinm`. f32 vs double return types matter (`frsp` or not).
- **Epilogue tell**: `lwz r0,20(r1)` *before* the r30/r31 restores means the function returns a value.
- **Constructors / destructors**: write a base ctor call, vtable store and field inits as plain C. For `new T[n]` with `__ct__9CXXXFv/__dt__9CXXXFv` relocs use `struct CXXX { CXXX(); ~CXXX(); }`; extab layout may still differ.
- **Destructor pattern**: `if (p) { …; if (f > 0) __dl__FPv(p); } return p;` with `s16 f`. For C++ destructors with virtual calls take `u8 *p` and cast; typing the param as `V*` grows the frame.
- **Static initializers**: `__register_global_object` / `__construct_array` / cos-sinit families need `__declspec(section ".ctors") static void (*const ctor_X)(void) = fn_X;` (`addctors.py`).
- **Thunks**: `asm` functions with `nofralloc` (`addi r3,r3,-N; b tgt`).
- **Loop hoisting**: `#pragma opt_loop_invariants off` stops `p+K` being hoisted out of parse loops.
- **Forwarded parameters**: a temporary in r6/r7 while r4/r5 are never set before a call means the function passes its own parameters through (`fn_80272F28(p, a, b)` calls `fn_801AA960(x, a, b)`). Add the parameters.
- **Scheduling only**: identical instructions in a different order (e.g. a void function whose epilogue restores r31 before `lwz r0`) can need `#pragma scheduling off` … `#pragma scheduling reset` around the function.
- **Byte locals**: `u8` vs `s8`/`char`/`int` for a loaded byte changes register allocation even when the instructions are the same.

- **More GC/1.3.2 tricks that matched large game functions** (library agent, 2026-10-22):
  - In C++, `int` vs `s32` (`long`) on a parameter can change register coloring.
  - Unfolded arithmetic in the original (`mulli 3; slwi 2`) comes from a repeated block in an inline helper.
  - A float the original reloads at every use is `extern volatile f32`.
  - String pools with gaps: a padding array plus a dead static function holding the filler literals.
  - State-table switch walkers: tables as static arrays at their real offsets, and the loop written as
    `for (q = b + idx; t > 0; q++) { if (*q > t) break; t -= b[idx++]; }`.
  - Virtual calls that load through r12 only come out of C++ with real virtual classes.

## Linking units whose code already matches

Many NonMatching units have every function at 100% and only fail the unit check (functions plus section layout vs
`build/GHYE6S/obj/<unit>.o`, see `tools/rea/layout.py`) or the C++ conversion. Check these before decompiling new code:

- **extab garbage**: some actions (e.g. DELETEPOINTERCOND) end in two uninitialized bytes; the original has `b1 02` (or
  `b0 02`). Add `extab_padding=[0xB1, 0x02]` to the unit's `Object(...)`; the `mwcc_extab` rule (`dtk extab clean`)
  then reproduces them. dtk consumes the padding bytes once over the whole table, so with several such actions repeat
  the pair once per action (`extab_padding=[0xB1, 0x02, 0xB1, 0x02]`).
- **C units that need `-use_lmw_stmw on`**: `tools/rea/apply_q.py` drops the flag for `.c` candidates, so a C match
  that uses `stmw`/`lmw` silently fails in the build. Convert such units to C++ with both flags (24 units on
  2026-10-21 matched only this way).
- **Missing `.ctors`/`.dtors` entries** for static initializers (`addctors.py`).
- **C to C++ conversion**: any integer cast on an argument (`(int)(p)`, even `(s32)` on an `s32`, or an implicit
  conversion through an `int` prototype) changes register allocation and saved registers in C++ mode. Give callees
  pointer prototypes and keep argument types equal to parameter types. C++ also needs explicit casts for `void *`
  results (`__nw__FUl`, `NULL`), and calls through `(RT (*)())` casts need their parameter list.
- **12-byte copies** in C++ must be `struct { u32 v[3]; }` (a struct of three scalars is copied memberwise).
- **Comparisons are `bool`** in C++: `return a == b;` from an `s32` function becomes `cntlzw; extrwi`; use a `bool`
  return or `x ? 1 : 0`, and `bool` locals for stored comparisons.
- **Function order** in the object follows source order (asm thunks included).
- **Dead functions**: a function only referenced by its own unit (e.g. by its jump table) is dead-stripped once the
  unit links from C; add it to `force_active` in `config/GHYE6S/config.yml`.
- **Literals** the original takes from named `.sdata`/`.sdata2` objects must use those externs (`extern const f64
  lbl_X;`, `extern char lbl_X[6];`, sized so small-data addressing is used).
- **Jump table at 4 mod 8**: a unit whose `.data` (a switch jump table) starts at 4 mod 8 cannot link alone, since
  the linker 8-aligns each object's `.data`. When the object in front of it is the class vtable sitting in a
  synthetic `jt_*` data unit, move the vtable into the unit (shrink the `jt_*` range, extend the unit's `.data` in
  splits.txt) and define it in the source before the functions: `void *lbl_X[7] = { 0, 0, (void *)fn_A, ... };`.
  When it is another function's jump table, the two units were one TU and need merging.
- **Shared compiler constants**: two adjacent units that reference the same `.sdata2` int-to-float double (or a
  string pool in `.rodata`) were one TU. Merge them and give the merged unit that range.
- **Mislabeled switch cases**: when only the jump table differs, the case labels can be attached to the wrong
  blocks (e.g. globals shifted by one case); the code bytes still match.
- **A failed link leaves the old main.dol in place**, so `sha1sum -c` still says OK. Check that ninja exited 0
  before trusting the sha (or delete `build/GHYE6S/main.dol` first).
- **Linker hangs forever** (`mwldeppc` at 100% CPU; a normal link takes about 15 s): an object whose `.text` starts with
  a global function and a global label at offset 0 (e.g. `SystemCallVector` and `__OSSystemCallVectorStart` in
  OSSync). Make the function `scope:local` in symbols.txt. To find the culprit, link with `timeout` while leaving out
  one object at a time from `build/GHYE6S/main.elf.rsp`.

## Exception-table constructs (C++, GC/1.3.2)

The decoded extab in `build/GHYE6S/asm/<unit>.s` says which C++ construct the original used:

- **DELETEPOINTER** on the `new` result: `p = new C;` with `sizeof(C)` equal to the `__nw__FUl` size and a ctor
  (often inline) holding the code inside `if (p)`. A plain-named base ctor is called as `fn_X(this)` first in it; a
  mangled one (`__ct__9CXXXXFv`) comes from deriving from `struct CXXXX { CXXXX(); };` (empty bases take no space).
- **DESTROYBASE / DESTROYMEMBER / DESTROYMEMBERARRAY**: real base classes, member objects and member arrays whose
  destructors are the Dtor symbols. Actions only cover throwing calls after the subobject is built, so a missing
  action does not mean there is no destructor. Several DESTROYMEMBER at one offset = a union of class-type members.
- **DESTROYBASE at `0x8(SP)`** (`06 00 0008`) for `new Derived` with an inline ctor: `#pragma optEH2 on` for the file
  (or `throw()` on the callees inside the inlined ctor body). Without it GC/1.3.2 names the register (`06 80 001f`).
  Don't use the pragma where the original names a register: under it multi-call ctor bodies also get the stack form.
- **DESTROYLOCAL**: `__ct__9CX(&obj); … __dt__9CX(&obj, -1);` is a local `CX obj;`.
- **Plain vtable labels**: MWCC puts the vptr after the data members of the class that introduces it. For a vtable named
  `lbl_X`, store it from a holder: an empty base whose inline ctor stores `lbl_X` at the vptr offset (when stored before
  all member ctors), or a member `struct V { u8 *vt; V() { vt = lbl_X; } }` (when stored between base and members).
- **Plain-named destructors** (`dtor_XXXX`, `fn_XXXX`) in extab cannot be produced: MWCC has no aliasing. Rename them
  (symbols.txt + sources, as `tools/rea/cxx.py apply` does) to the mangled destructor of their class. A class's dtor
  sits right before its ctor in this binary (dtor address + size = ctor address), which tells you the class name.
- `__ct__9CXXXXFs` is the ctor of a class with a virtual base (hidden `short` in r4); MWCC mangles it `…Fv`.

## Known blockers (skip unless you crack them)

- **Inlined base ctor** `mr r30,r3; mr r0,r30; mr r31,r0` (e.g. `__ct__9C80155460Fv`, `80155E80`, `8020E5C0`). Inline ctors, multiple inheritance and member-object variants were all tried without success.
- **Member ctor held in r30** (e.g. `__ct__9C80064474Fv` family, `80239B40`, `8022F98C`).
- **Zero-then-assign** at +40/+44 after `fn_801EF9A0` (`li r3,0; lwz r4,8(q); stw r3,44; …`): e.g. `fn_801DEE18`, `801E812C`, `801DE80C`, `801E0384`, `801E7D14`.
- **Descriptor init / factory family** (about 20 functions near 0x8004–0x800B): they address the unit's own `.data` through a section-base register, so they need that data defined as statics in the same TU. The same goes for pooled string literals (`fn_80068D50`, `80046BB8`, `80267CF8`, …).
- **Local objects whose dtor is `dtor_XXXX`** (`fn_8012D774`, `80138AA8`, `8013EF3C`): need the dtor renamed (see "Plain-named destructors" above).
- **Symbol renames** (done): 41 plain-named ctors/dtors used by exception tables were renamed to mangled names (recorded in `tools/rea/renames.json`), which matched the `fn_8012CF08` family, `fn_80105B88` and 30 other units. `fn_80117DC4` matched without a rename (inline ctor/dtor calling the two literal names). 0x80118DE0 is now `__dt__9C80118E78Fv`, which freed `__dt__9C8011F770Fv` for `dtor_8011F6FC`. `fn_8013D770` still needs renames for its four member-array classes.
- **Switch jump tables** (data must match too): `fn_8012F7F8`, `8010CF00`, `801FA224`, `8021C124`. The others in the old list are linked (vtable moves, case relabeling, extab padding). Units whose jump table follows another function's jump table (`800447E8`, `800501FC`, `80052028`, `8008CA0C`, `80090C68`, `800919A4`) need merging with undecompiled neighbours.
- **Near misses (scheduling or register allocation only)**: `fn_80153D4C`, `8017CB04`, `801BB258`, `801BCA04`, `8016E4D4`, `801B2504`, `80191360`, `8019F53C`, `8019FCB0`, `801A0424`, `80158E34`, `801547BC`, `80149C68`, `80186960`, `8022202C`, `801DF270`, `8023C3F4`, `80213F6C`, `8021F4A4`, `80240948`, `801C9C40`, `8024973C`, `801F13F0`, `801C1774`, `8003C674`, `8003C778`, `801135BC`, `80112FD4`, `8010E280`, `80112190`, `80102730`, `800F9838`, `80112698`, `8011FA98`, `800FA69C`, `80071CE8`.
- **Factory `new` functions** (solved): real classes with an inline derived ctor storing the vtable label by hand, plus `#pragma optEH2 on` for the stack-slot DESTROYBASE (see above), matched 37 factories with base `C8024262C` and others.
- `fn_800F8410` only matches with GC/2.0; its unit would need a compiler switch.

(`awk '!/^#/{print $2}' tools/decomp_agent/claimed_by_library_agent.txt >> $DECOMP_SCRATCH/skip.txt`) and the two
loops won't overlap. Pull often: both agents commit to `main`.

The library-unit agent works in this repo too and commits its own batches. Commit only your own units: `git add` specific paths rather than `-A`. If a library unit shows up as modified in `git status`, leave it alone.

- collaborator (2026-11-12, evening): **six more to-do functions linked**, each a one-unit link whose data already
  matched (45 of the 87 are now in linked units; NonMatching units below `0x80165000`: 62 -> 56). Exact: fn_8013CFCC,
  fn_80085E44, fn_80087E20. Schedule-equivalent: fn_8005D9EC, fn_8007AC1C, fn_80030610.
  - **A register-colouring model that predicted five of them** (GC/1.3.2 and 2.0p1): unnamed expression temporaries are
    coloured first, the LATEST-CREATED first (creation = evaluation order of statements and operands); named locals
    come after them; every value takes the lowest free volatile FPR (f0 up) or GPR. So: write down the register of
    each value in the target, derive which value must be coloured before which (a value in f2 needs neighbours that
    already hold f0 and f1), then pick which sub-expressions are named locals, their declaration order and the
    statement order. What that meant in practice:
    - cross product blocks (fn_80085E44, fn_80087E20, fn_8007AC1C): store the results straight from their expressions
      in x, y, z order (the last-created z result takes f0); a product shared by two results is a named local declared
      BEFORE the vector components when the original gives it a fresh, higher register
      (`f32 t1; f32 vy = v.y; f32 vx = v.x; f32 vz = v.z; n.x = vy * K1 - (t1 = vz * K0); n.y = -(vx * K1 - t1);
      n.z = vx * K0 - vy * K0;` with x and y as volatile stores and z plain = exact). Two equal products the original
      keeps apart: swap the operands of one (CSE is syntactic; the checker accepts the commutative swap).
    - fn_8005D9EC: `f32 m, z;` (constant first) gives K in f3 and the difference in f4.
    - fn_80030610: `char *pool = lbl_X;` declared BEFORE `f32 dt = lbl_Y->f14;` creates the address temporary first, so
      the global pointer is coloured first and takes r4. Half of the 120 declaration orders pass, exactly those.
    - A named local initialised by a plain load is propagated and behaves like an unnamed temporary; read it through
      a volatile cast to keep it a named local (fn_8003BC2C scale block: `f32 z, y, x, k;` with `k = *(volatile f32 *)&K`).
  - **A temporary left in an argument register before a call can be a missing argument**: fn_8013CFCC kept the DQT
    precision in r5 because `fn_801F4C84` takes it as a third parameter (`equivcheck` words this as "r5 (callee
    input) holds a different value at the call"). Claude workers: fn_801F4C84 is in your range and reads r5.
  - fn_8013CFCC, rest: all four "more data?" tests are one inline with an unnamed result, which gives them one shared
    register (a named flag inside the inline is a separate variable per site); `if (x) return 1; return 0;` is folded
    into a branchless `x != 0`, the form `if (!x) goto z; return 1; z: return 0;` keeps the original's branch.
    `equivcheck` cannot follow this function's jump table (the base register is loaded in the loop header, not in the
    block of the `bctr`), so it could only link as an exact match.
  - A store through another local's address with an out-of-range index (`((f32 *)&c)[4]`, `((f32 *)col)[9]`) is a
    "may alias anything" store: a later load of a global stays behind it. That is what delays the second constant in
    fn_8007AC1C and keeps the next block's constant out of fn_8003BC2C's scale block.
  - Not solved: fn_80088B54 (same cross block as fn_8007AC1C, but its components are loaded from the stack; five
    sweeps, about 600 variants: the second constant is always loaded too early, so `d.x * K3` is scheduled before the
    z result and the registers shift), fn_8003BC2C (draft improved, the scale block is exact; left: the second

- collaborator (2026-11-12, night): **fn_80095770 linked** (schedule-equivalent; 46 of the 87 at that point), and the colouring rule from the previous entry checked on small test functions
  (GC/1.3.2 and 2.0p1, `-O4,p`):
  - Volatile FPRs: unnamed temporaries first (latest-created first), then NAMED locals in DECLARATION order, each
    taking the lowest free register. Four locals that are all live together get f1..f4 in declaration order whatever
    the order of their assignments. So for a block that only differs in FP registers: declare the named locals in
    the order of the ORIGINAL's registers, lowest first (fn_80095770: `float bz0, by0, bx0, k0, pz, py, px;` and
    `float dx, dy, dz, bz, by, bx;`).
  - An inline function's parameters and locals are separate variables created at the expansion. A local of the caller
    that is only handed to an inline (`Cross(&c, &u, &d)`, or scalars passed to a helper) is coloured in the inline
    parameter's place, not in its own declaration place: 720 declaration orders could not move b.x and d.z in
    fn_80095770 until the first cross product was written out in the function itself.
  - A named local initialised by a plain read of a GLOBAL scalar is not reliably a named local (in fn_80088B54 the two
    constants kept f4/f5 through all 24 declaration orders); read it through a volatile cast when its position matters.
  - Callee-saved GPRs (r31 down), in STRAIGHT-LINE test functions: locals in declaration order first, then the
    parameters from LAST to FIRST. With a loop it is not declaration order any more: in
    `q = getq(p); n = cnt(p); for (i = 0, off = 0; i < n; i++, off += 20) work(p, q->f10 + off, i);` the bound `n`
    gets r31 and `q` r30 however they are declared, then `i` / `off` in declaration order, then the parameter (an
    offset the compiler derives from `i * 20` takes the same place as an explicit `off`). So for saved registers
    declaration order only settles ties between variables of the same kind. A
    parameter copied into a local (same or other pointer type, through an identity inline, a base-class or
    first-member conversion, a reference, a member function's `this`) is still coloured as the parameter; only a
    local with a real computation (`p + 4`, `&p->member` at a non-zero offset, a loaded pointer) is a local of its
    own. That is why fn_8003CAD8 (original: first parameter r31, lookup result r30, second parameter r29) does not
    move: 30 forms give the result r31, `add` r30, the object r29.
  - **fn_8005A874 linked too** (register-equivalent; 47 of the 87, 54 NonMatching units left below `0x80165000`).
    Its 70 differing lines were a rotation of three saved registers, which rule d accepts as a renaming; the only
    real obstacle was the spot the checker named: four independent byte stores (`b10[8] = 0; b10[5] = 'S';
    b10[6] = j / 10 + '0'; b10[7] = j % 10 + '1';`). Their temporaries are coloured latest-created first, so the
    statement order decides which of r0/r3/r5 holds the zero, the 'S' and the quotient: the order [6], [7], [5], [8]
    is exact there (11 of the 24 orders pass). Before giving up on a "register-only" function, read the address in
    the `equivcheck --list` message and sweep the statement order of that block alone.
  - What the rule does not give is the SCHEDULE. Where the original keeps a load of a global behind a store, or
    loads stack parameters right after the parameter copies, the instruction order has to be right first, because
    the live ranges (and so the registers) follow from it. Still open for that reason: fn_80088B54 and fn_800AAC34
    (the second constant is loaded 3 slots after a stack store in the original, earlier in every form tried),
    fn_8015EED0 (stack parameter loaded at entry into r11, then `mr r3,r11`; `#pragma scheduling 604` / `7450` are
    2 lines closer, none match), fn_8003BC2C (second int->float result in f1: no spelling of the conversion or of
    the product puts it there, 40 forms).
  - **Proposal for `equivcheck.py` rule f (measured in a private copy, nothing changed in the repo):** a register
    that still holds an unmodified incoming argument (r3-r10 at entry, or a plain `mr` copy of one; a call clears
    r0, r3-r12) cannot hold an address inside the frame the function allocates, nor of the back-chain word written
    by its `stwu r1,-N(r1)`, nor of the LR save word at N+4: the caller computed that value before the frame
    existed. Treating a load/store through such a base as disjoint from own-frame accesses `[0, N)`, from the
    `stwu` itself and from the store at `N+4(r1)` makes fn_800FFA4C and fn_8014FABC pass (schedule- and
    register-equivalent; their `lfs fX,0(r4)` sits above the `stwu` in the original). It needs no "no frame address
    escapes" condition, so it also applies to functions that take `addi rX,r1,..`. Both units then still need their
    0.0f in `.sdata2`.
